‘Bio 


KSL/BYU Partnership 


The BYU Athletic Department and KSL Radio and 
Television have entered into a unique new agreement 
to market broadcast rights to BYU athletic events. 

The agreement, effective for at least three years, is 
designed to provide advertisers with an opportunity to 
reach BYU's large sports audience via electronic 
media and in-arena promotions. 

Under the new arrangement, sponsors may contact 
a single source, KSL/BYU Sports, for advertising op- 
portunities associated with BYU athletic events. 

Advertisers may purchase marketing "packages" 
that include television and radio exposure, as well as 
opportunities for in-arena exposure at athletic events. 

The agreement is unique because BYU and KSL 
will share the advertising revenue derived from the 
partnership, whereas most universities negotiate a 
rights’ fee on a per-game or a season basis. 

"As far as we know, this cooperative approach to 
marketing broadcast rights for university athletics has 
not been tried before," says Glen Tuckett, BYU Ath- 
letic Director. "But with BYU and KSL continuing their 
40-plus years of working together, we can make it a 
win for BYU, KSL, our fans and our advertisers." 

The agreement between KSL and BYU affects local 
broadcast rights only. BYU is a member of the Col- 
lege Football Association and will honor all television 
and radio contractual obligations with that organiza- 
tion and with the Western Athletic Conference. 


KSL TV 

KSL Television, the CBS affiliate in 
Salt Lake City, has been televising 
BYU football games on a regular basis 
since 1984. Craig Bolerjack, a 
graduate of the University of Kansas, 
provides play-by-play for the fans. 
Marc Lyons, a BYU quarterback from 
1967-69 and a former coach at Olym- 
pus High in Salt Lake City, handles the 
color commentary. 


Games Via Phone 

For fans outside the Intermountain area who can't 
hear the games on KSL or one of its network stations, 
they can listen by telephone. 

Once again, TRZ Sports Services, Inc., in Akron, 
Ohio, will provide the KSL broadcast of BYU games 
via telephone. 

The toll-free number to reach TRZ via a Touch-tone 
phone in the United States or Canada is 1-800-225- 
5448. After giving a Visa or Mastercard number, 
callers will be connected to the broadcast. 


LB 


Craig Bolerjack 


Depending on how long they listen, fans are billed 
between 20 cents and 50 cents per minute. The cost 
of listening to a game for three hours is $36.50. 


KSL Radio 

For more than 40 years, BYU fans 
have been following Cougar athletics 
via 50,000-watt KSL Radio (1160 AM), 
located in Salt Lake City. 

The "Voice" of the Cougars has 
been play-by-play man Paul James, a 
veteran broadcaster with 41 years’ ex- 
perience. An Ogden native and 
University of Utah graduate, James 
began radio work in 1950. 

For six years, he broadcast Utah basketball and 
football games before joining the staff at KSL Radio 
and Television in July of 1965. James has been at the 
microphone calling BYU games for the past 26 years. 

Jim Braden is the sideline reporter for KSL Radio, 
and Chris Tunis mans Sports Central in the studio 
during broadcasts. 


la, 


Paul James 


KBYU-TV 

The tradition continues between KBYU and Cougar 
football. All home games are televised on a tape 
delay basis by Channel 11 with Jay Monsen doing the 
play-by-play. 

An experienced sports crew and state-of-the-art 
equipment have enabled KBYU to develop a reputa- 
tion for outstanding coverage of football, basketball 
and other major "remote" events in the western U.S. 

Blue and White Network broadcasts (BYU and Air 
Force football) are produced for television by KBYU- 
TV's national production crews. 

KBYU has won numerous awards for its sports 
productions and is frequently employed by ESPN, 
TBS Sports, Prime Ticket, the Utah Jazz Network, 
USA Network, SportsChannel and well as local af- 
filiates of national networks. 

KBYU owns and operates a fully-equipped, 48-foot, 
remote production truck, designed by experienced 
professionals to meet the most demanding standards. 

Traveling with an experienced team of engineers 
and technicians, the KBYU truck has a full comple- 
ment of on-board audio and video equipment with 
state-of-the-art cameras, long lenses, one-inch and 
3/4-inch tape machines, video monitoring, specials ef- 
fects, Chyron graphics and a complete internal com- 
munication system. 

Questions can be directed to Mikel Minor, 
Television Sports Manager: (801) 378-0050.

Outlook 

After finishing in the nation's Top 
Four the past four seasons, an- 
ticipation runs high for a deter- 
mined run at the national 
championship. 

The schedule isn’t easy but 
three excellent recruiting years 
plus the 10-2 "rebuilding" season 
of 1990 has left the Seminoles 
deep in talent. 

The entire backfield is intact--three deep at each 
spot--led by quarterback Casey Weldon, whose 152.7 
passing efficiency ranks second to Ty Detmer (155.9) 
among returning signal callers. 

Fullback Edgar Bennett can do it all and tailback 
Amp Lee was MVP against Penn State in the Block- 
buster Bowl. The entire offensive line from the bowl 
returns. 

The tight end and flanker are gone but FSU has a 
reputation for great receivers. The only other en- 
couraging news for opponents is that the placekicker 
and deep snapper are new. 

Inside linebackers Marvin Jones and Kirk Car- 
ruthers are exceptional and the three down linemen 
return. One cornerback, Terrell Buckley, is an All- 
American and great punt returner and the other, Errol 
McCorvey, is a three-year starter. 


Series Notes 

This will be the first meeting between the schools 
and Coach Bowden says, "I must be crazy after 
seeing what they did to Miami last year. 

"But if you’re ever going to play in one of those pre- 
season games you want to do it when you've got 
some experienced players returning. I'd say this 
would be the year if there ever was one." 

Bowden, with six more years coaching than LaVell 
Edwards, has 30 more victories to put him in second 
place (205) compared to Edwards’ 175.

Outlook 

Quarterback Tommy Maddox 
and strong safety Matt Darby hope 
to lead the Bruins back into conten- 
tion for a high Pac-10 finish. 

Last year UCLA lost every other 
week to wind up tied for sixth in the 
tough Pac-10. However, only the 
Bruins were still in contention for a 
bowl game until suffering two los- 
ses in November--by four points to 
Oregon and by three to Southern Cal. 

Of the Bruins’ 54 lettermen, 33 started at least one 
game. The chief target for Maddox will be junior wide 
receiver Sean LaChapelle. Junior inside linebacker Ar- 
nold Ale also is an outstanding player. 

Three starters are back in the secondary and 
shoulder surgery on defensive end Mike Chalenski 
should strengthen a defense whose main losses were 
at outside linebacker. 

The offensive line returns which should help a 
group of experienced running backs. 


Series Notes 

Terry Donahue’s seven straight bowl victories from 
1982-88 tie him with Bobby Bowden and Pat Dye for 
the best percentage (.750) in bowl games. 

His 116-51-8 record makes him the eighth win- 
ningest (.686) coach in Division IA. He is 2-1 against 
BYU. The Cougars won the first game in the series, 
37-35, in Pasadena (1983), where Casey Tiumalu 
rushed for 137 yards and BYU sacked quarterback 
Steve Bono eight times. 

The 1985 game also was shown on ESPN and that 
time it was the Bruins’ turn to win a close one, 27-24, 
in Provo. A 62-yard bomb late in the game ended a 
25-game BYU winning streak--the nation’s longest. 

The third game was a rout with UCLA crushing the 
Cougars, 31-10, in the 1986 Freedom Bowl at 
Anaheim. Gaston Green rushed for 266 yards to help 
UCLA break open a 7-3 halftime lead.

Joe Paterno 


Outlook 

Loaded on defense but some 
question marks on offense sum- 
marizes the 1991 Nittany Lions, 
who rebounded from an 0-2 start to 
reel off nine straight victories, win 
their 20th Lambert Trophy and 
finish 10th and 11th in the polls. 

Even though Penn State lost 
four of its five offensive linemen 
and the top two running backs, 
three-year starter Tony Sacca 
returns at quarterback. 

All four linebackers return with star status attached 
to the inside duo of Mark D’Onofrio and Keith 
Goganious. 

Also back are two down linemen and two defensive 
backs, including Darren Perry, who had seven inter- 
ceptions. 

Other highly regarded players in the secondary are 
cornerback Leonard Humphries, who have off-season 
surgery, and cornerback Derek Bochna, who was sen- 
sational in a part-time starting role as a freshman. 


Series Notes 

The only previous meeting resulted in a 50-39 
Penn State victory in the 1989 Holiday Bowl. 

Ty Detmer's 576 yards passing--the most ever ina 
bowl game--weren't enough to offset 186 yards rush- 
ing by co-MVP Blair Thomas. 

The Cougars got within 41-39 with two minutes left 
to play only to have a two-point conversion pass 
returned 102 yards by Andre Collins. 

BYU then drove from its own 12 to the Penn State 
38 only to have the ball stripped on a an apparent 
sack and returned for the deciding TD by Gary Brown. 

The rematch pits the second and third winningest 
coaches in college football, Joe Paterno (.789) and 
LaVell Edwards (.747).

61 


Outlook 

Air Force, which has led the WAC 
in rushing every year since 1962, 
returns its leading rusher, Jason 
Jones (598 yards), and quarterback 
Rob Perez. All but one offensive 
lineman is back from the team which 
shocked Ohio State. 

The Falcons were the only team in 
the country not to throw a touchdown 
pass but placekicker Joe Wood has 
connected on 67 of 69 PATs and 
punter Jason Christ averaged 42.6. 

Both halfbacks will be new but there are a lot of 
candidates, including Ron Gray, who started last year 
at quarterback. 

The defense is led by linebacker Vergil Simpson, 
tackle Chris Baker and cornerback Carlton McDonald. 
Two other defensive backs, Shannon Yates and Eldrick 
Hill, also return. 

The Falcons must replace its two leading tacklers, 
linebackers Brain Hill and J.T. Tokish, but there are a lot 
of experienced players back. 


Series Notes 

BYU has won 12 of the 13 meetings and eight straight. 
Air Force notched its only win in 1982, when the Falcons 
upended the Cougars, 39-38, in the first game played 
at the expanded stadium. 

Last year, with a wind chill factor near zero, Ty Detmer 
completed 30 of 43 passes in three quarters as the 
Cougars went up, 47-7, and won, 54-7. 

Air Force has been to bowl games in seven of the last 
nine years, missing only 1986 and 1988. 

In 1985, the Falcons were 10-0 and ranked fourth in 
the nation when they lost to the Cougars, 27-20. 

Two years ago, a national television audience saw 
Stacey Corley return kickoffs 99 and 85 yards. 


Rob Perez

Chuck Shelton 


Outlook 

The Aggies won four of their last 
five games to tie for second in the 
Big West at 5-5-1, their first non- 
losing season since 1981. 

Now with 10 starters back on of- 
fense, USU hopes to challenge for 
league honors and the host spot in 
the California Bowl. 

The optimism starts with Roger 
Grant, the nation's number-two 
returning rusher (124.55 yards per 
game). 

Quarterback Ron Lopez passed for nearly 2,000 
yards in half a season as the starter. He was pushed 
in spring drills by transfer Greg Angelovic. And don't 
forget split end Rod Moore, who had 123 yards last 
year against the Cougars. 

The only loss on offense was tight end Ryan Duve 
and there are three JC players vying for his spot. 
Also, the league's top punter, Rusty Carlsen is back 
along with the leader in field goals, Doug Beach. 

Veteran Del Lyles anchors the defense but he’s the 
only linebacker returning. The three down linemen 
return along with two defensive backs and backup 
free safety Damon Smith, who led the league in inter- 
ceptions as a freshman last year. 


Series Notes 

Utah State still leads the series and has given the 
Cougars more trouble than any other team during La- 
Vell Edwards’ tenure, winning five times. 

A BYU victory this year would even the series after 
USU dominated the first 20 years and the Cougars 
have dominated the last 20. 

BYU has won eight straight and 11 of 12. Utah 
State’s last win in Provo was 1978 (24-7). 

Last year BYU bounced back from a 3-0 first 
quarter deficit for a 45-10 victory as Ty Detmer threw 
five touchdown passes (560 yards) and Josh Arnold 
returned an interception for a score.

Series Notes

year’s 30-10 victory.

as Perez took over early from an injured Howard 
Gasser.

yards to help the Cougars bounce back from a 10-7 
deficit. 

The BYU-UTEP series started in 1946 with Miner 
victories coming in 1948, 1949, 1967, 1968, 1970 and 
1985. The two teams tied in 1964. 

Four times against UTEP, BYU has racked up over 
60 points in a game, and the all-time BYU high output 
of 83 points came against the Miners in 1980. The 83 
points is also the most ever given up by a UTEP team.

64 


Outlook 

A schedule which includes lowa 
and Notre Dame and trips to 
Laramie, Fort Collins and Provo 
will test the Rainbow Warriors. 

Having running back Jamal 
Farmer and slotback Jeff Sydner 
return certainly will help as will an 
expected strong defense. 

Hawaii has led the WAC in total 
defense four of the last five years. 
Safety Tony Pang-Kee leads an excellent secondary 
and tackles Akili Calhoun and Maa Tanuvasa are 
good ones. 

Returning from injuries a year ago are kicker Jason 
Elam, defensive lineman Delmar Johnson and 
linebacker Herman Talley. 

The biggest question is how well Michael Carter or 
Ivin Jasper can replace Garrett Gabriel, who set 32 
school records and was eighth in passing efficiency. 


Series Notes 

The frustration of 10 straight losses to BYU was 
vented the last two years when the Rainbows romped 
to victories of 56-14 and 59-28. The party was over 
early in 1989 as Hawaii scored three quick touch- 
down with a no-huddle offense and was in front, 35-7, 
at halftime. 

Last year the Cougars had been excited all after- 
noon about the WAC’s first ever Heisman Trophy win- 
ner but it was Hawaii which celebrated at night. 

From a 7-7 deadlock, the Rainbows jumped on top 
35-14 at halftime. 

Ty Detmer did finish with 5,188 passing yards--the 
most in the history of college football--but for the only 
time during the season he was outpassed. Gabriel 
threw for 359 yards compared to 319 for Detmer. 

BYU had been 9-0 since Hawaii joined the WAC. 
and the Cougars had claimed eight in a row at 
Honolulu, where the Rainbows had last won in 1974.

65 


Outlook 

An almost entire new staff, in- 
cluding former BYU assistant head 
coach Dave Kragthorpe, will try to 
get the Lobos untracked in a year 
when only three league games are 
at home. 

New Mexico will go with a Run 
and Shoot offense but most of the 
deiense must be replaced. 

There are so many question 
marks that Jeremy Leach, sixth on 
the WAC’s all-time passing yardage list, might give 
way to Marcus Goodloe, who started the final two 
games last year. 

They can throw to sophomore standout Carl 
Winston and tight end Jerone Williams. 

The defense might not be as bad as it looks on 
paper. There are lettermen who played a lot in 
reserve roles and there are a number of junior college 
transfers.

also return. 


Steve Slater 


Series Notes 

Last year in the Homecoming game at Provo, Andy 
Boyce caught nine passes for 235 yards and Derwin 
Gray intercepted three passes as the Cougars won, 
55-31. 

BYU managed two touchdowns and a field goal just 
before halftime to break a 21-21 tie. 

In 1964, New Mexico ruined BYU's inaugural ap- 
pearance in Cougar Stadium, winning 26-14. 

A year later, BYU clinched its first-ever WAC cham- 
pionship with a win over the Lobos 42-8. Sadly, the 
win was marked by tragedy as a plane carrying 13 
Cougar fans from Salt Lake to Albuquerque crashed 
at the Point-of-the-Mountain on the day of the game. 

In 1973, Jay Miller set an NCAA record that still 
stands with 22 receptions against New Mexico.

Outlook

in Provo last year, the Rams 
finished the season with wins over 
Hawaii and Oregon--teams BYU 
couldn't beat.

in 42 years was achieved with a 
school record 2,827 yards rushing y 
and 5,250 total yards. \ 

Coach Bruce ranks ninth among Keer vercuge 
active coaches in winning percentage (.660) and 11th 
in total wins. Last year the Rams were ninth national- 
ly in turnover margin and 18th in total offense. 

The WAC’s leading rusher, Brian Copeland, is back 
along with the team's top receiver, Greg Primus, and 
quarterback Kevin Verdugo.

great secondary.

Bruce has some transfers and redshirts who could 
make CSU a factor again in the conference race.

to overwhelm the Rams in Provo. BYU intercepted 
five passes and linebacker Scott Giles, making his 
first start due to an injury to Jared Leavitt, made six 
tackles. 

The CSU game started a stretch in which punter 
Earl Kauffman had to kick only once in 12 quarters. 

The Rams’ 24-20 come-from-behind victory over 
BYU in 1986 deadlocked the series but four straight 
Cougar wins have put BYU back on top.

Joe Tiller 


67 


Outlook 

Joe Tiller, an assistant under 
Paul Roach in 1987 and 1988, 
returns to Laramie after two years 
as an assistant at Washington 
State. He was head coach of the 
CFL's Calgary Stampeders in 1976. 

He inherits Wyoming's all-time Py 
leading passer in Tom Corontzos j 4 r 
and kicker/punter Sean Fleming, a Poul Wallace 
three-year regular. 

The biggest losses were on the offensive line and 
among the linebackers. Wyoming thinks the defen- 
sive front will be the strength despite the loss of 
Player of the Year Mitch Donahue. The secondary 
also is solid. 

The punt and kickoff return abilities of wide receiver 
Robert Rivers are another plus. Still, the Cowboys 
have holes to fill and remain a question mark going 
into the 1991 season under a new coach. 


Series Notes 

Mike Salido rushed for 96 yards and Ty Detmer 
threw for 484 a year ago as BYU disappointed 
Wyoming's first sellout crowd with a 45-14 victory. 

The Cougars went in front, 24-0, early in the 
second quarter as a nationwide ESPN audience 
looked on. 

The teams have split their last four games but the 
Cougars have won seven of the last nine and 11 of 
14. Akey game in BYU's national championship year 
was coming from behind to beat Wyoming, 41-38. 

A bitter loss in the rivalry came in 1976, when 
Wyoming Coach Fred Akers devised a quarterback 
draw play which kept the Cougars off balance and 
resulted in a 34-29 victory, sending the Cowboys to 
the Fiesta Bowl. 

In his four seasons Coach Roach was 35-15 and 
went to three bowl games.

catches for 116 yards for SDSU. 

San Diego won the first game of the series in 1947 
and took two of the first three. But BYU won nine ina 
row between 1976 and 1985.

Series Notes 

BYU overcame a 10-7 first quarter deficit in 1990 to 
take a 35-10 halftime lead and won, 45-22. 

Chris Smith caught eight passes for 125 yards and 
Brent Nyberg had four receptions for 116 yards. The 
win enabled BYU to clinch the WAC title and its 13th 
straight bowl appearance. 

Ty Detmer became the first junior to go over 10,000 
passing and the first quarterback ever to have back- 
to-back 4,000-yard seasons. 

BYU has won 10 of the last 11 games and 17 of 19 
under LaVell Edwards.

Thrifty Car Rental Holiday Bowl 


The Western Athletic Conference champion plays 
in the Thrifty Car Rental Holiday Bow! at Jack Murphy 
Stadium in San Diego, Calif. 

This year’s 14th annual Holiday Bowl, in the first 
year of a three-year national advertising agreement 
with Thrifty, takes place on Monday, Dec. 30. 

Kickoff is expected at either 4:30 p.m. or 5:30 p.m. 
Pacific Time. This will be only the second non-Friday 
game in bowl history. Last year's game was played 
on Saturday. 

It is played at the 60,409-seat stadium which 
hosted the 1988 Super Bowl. 

The bowl has been in existence since 1978, when 
Navy edged BYU, 23-16, in what was then called San 
Diego Stadium. The name was changed in 1981 to 
honor the San Diego Union sports editor who had led 
the campaign to have the stadium constructed in 
1967. Murphy died in 1980. 

In 1985, the stadium was expanded from 52,675 for 
football and a $6.5 million, 300,000 pound Diamond 
Vision scoreboard was added with a 66 by 100-foot 
scoreboard and screen for replays. 

WAC teams have played in the Holiday Bowl every 
year except 1985, when Arizona State and Arkansas 
played. Since then, the bowl has resumed its tie-in 
with the WAC and the current contract runs through 
the 1992 season. 

The WAC is 4-8 games with close games generally 
prevailing. An exception was last year’s 65-14 romp 
by Texas A&M over BYU. In 1989, Penn State scored 
in the final minutes on a 102-yard interception return 
and a 53-yard fumble recovery to beat BYU, 50-39. 

In 1986 and 1987, lowa defeated San Diego State 
(39-38) and Wyoming (20-19). Oklahoma State 
belted Wyoming, 62-14, in 1988. 

The second year (1979), BYU lost to Indiana, 38- 
37. In the Miracle Bowl of 1980, BYU scored three 
touchdowns in the final two minutes and 33 seconds 


al NS 


San Diego Jack Murphy Stadium 


to edge Southern Methodist, 46-45. 

In 1981, BYU beat Washington State, 38-36, but 
lost to Ohio State, 47-17, in 1982. In 1983, it was 
BYU 21, Missouri 17, and the Cougars defeated 
Michigan, 24-17, to win the national championship in 
1984. 

Even the non-WAC game was a squeaker with 
Arkansas nipping Arizona State, 18-17. 

A major change in the Holiday Bowl came in 1988, 
when Sea World became the sponsor and the bow! 
name was changed to Sea World Holiday Bowl. 

That relationship changed this year when negotia- 
tions for a new title sponsor were completed.

Cougar Bowl History 


Holiday Bowl XIII, 1990 
Texas A&M 65, BYU 14 
December 29 
San Diego 

Led by quarterback Bucky Richardson and All- 
American running back Darren Lewis, underdog Texas 
A&M was out to show it could play with the Cougars. 
The Aggies were able to prove that point and a lot 
more as they stunned the Cougars. 

Going into the game, the Cougars were fighting in- 
jury problems. Tony Crutchfield and Norm Dixon 
weren't able to suit up, and Ty Detmer was playing 
with a slight separation in his left shoulder. 

The second largest Holiday Bowl crowd ever 
(61,441) watched the Aggies march 80 yards in 13 
plays to take a 7-0 lead. The Cougars were able to tie 
the score on an eight-yard Ty Detmer to Chris Smith 
touchdown pass, but after that, the game was all Texas 
A&M. The Aggies went on to score 23 points in the 
second quarter en route to a 37-7 halftime lead. 

With the game out of hand, the big story was William 
Thomas’ crunching sack that separated Detmer’s right 
shoulder and put him out of the game early in the third 
quarter. After playing valiantly with one separation, this 
one proved to be too much. Detmer would have 
surgery a few days later to fix the damage. 

The Aggies continued to dominate by racking up 
nearly 200 yards rushing in the second half. The fact 
that Texas A&M's Lewis was still in the game with eight 
minutes to go and a 58-14 lead caused a slight stir 
among Cougar fans. A bright spot for BYU was Joe 
Evans' first touchdown pass as a Cougar quarterback. 

Texas A&M's Richardson would go on to be named 
the Offensive Player of the Game by racking up 344 
yards in total offense. Thomas was named the top 
defensive player with six tackles, two sacks and an in- 
terception. 

BYU finished the season ranked 17th by UPI, 20th 
by USA Today/CNN and 22nd by AP. The Cougars 
had been as high as fourth prior to their first loss. 

Obvious highlights were a win in BYU's first-ever 
meeting with a number-one team (Miami) and the first 
Heisman Trophy ever in the WAC.

Holiday Bowl XIl, 1989 


Penn State 50, BYU 39 
December 29 
San Diego 

Two very unusual plays late in the game kept BYU 
from toppling the East's best team, Lambert Trophy- 
winner Penn State. Two stunning defensive plays off- 
set 576 yards passing by Ty Detmer--the highest total 
ever in a bowl game. 

For his heroics, Detmer had the rare privilege of 
sharing Most Valuable Player honors despite being 
on the losing team. The other co-MVP was the Nit- 
tany Lions’ Blair Thomas, who rushed for 186 yards 
and picked up some key first downs for Joe Paterno’s 
team. 

BYU, which trailed, 41-26, with nine and a half 
minutes remaining, scored twice to cut the lead to 41- 
39 with two and a half minutes left to play. But a two- 
point conversion pass, which would have tied the 
game, was intercepted by linebacker Andre Collins 
and returned 102 yards to give Penn State two points. 

The Cougars tried an on-side kick but Penn State 
fell on the ball at the BYU 46-yard line. Two tackles 
behind the line of scrimmage by Bob Davis forced the 
Lions to punt and the Cougars got the ball at their 
own 12 with two minutes remaining. 

Passes to Brent Nyberg, Matt Bellini, Chris Smith 
and Andy Boyce moved the ball to the Penn State 38 
with a minute left to play. Then what most BYU fans 
thought was a sack by safety Gary Brown became a 
score for Penn State. He stripped the ball from Det- 
mer at the Penn State 47 and raced 53 yards with 45 
seconds left. 

The Cougars moved from their own 32 to the Penn 
State 40 in the final 30 seconds before time ran out. 

Penn State finished the season ranked 14th by UPI 
and 15th by AP and USA Today/CNN. BYU was 18th 
in the UPI poll and 22nd in the other two polls.

Freedom Bowl V, 1988 
BYU 20, Colorado 17 
December 29 
Anaheim 


A tough second-half defense, Ty Detmer coming off 


the bench to earn Most Valuable Player honors and 
two fourth-quarter field goals by Jason Chaffetz 
enabled the Cougars to upset Colorado. 

Cool weather (42 degrees) by southern California 
standards didn’t hamper diminutive Eric Bieniemy, 
who rushed 33 times for 144 yards and two touch- 
downs. 

But his heroics couldn't offset Detmer's 11 for 19 
and 129 yards in the second. Scott Peterson made a 
key pass interception to set up the game-winning field 
goal. 

It was an exciting contest for the 35,941 fans 
(49,694 tickets were sold) although a big show failed 
to materialize in a matchup of the nation’s top two 
punters. Colorado’s Keith English averaged only 39 
yards on two punts and BYU’s Pat Thompson had 
four punts for a 33.5 average. 

A fumble at midfield led to the Buffaloes’ first score 
and then Sean Covey found Mike Salido to tie the 
score. An interception in the end zone stopped a 
BYU drive early in the second quarter and the 
Cougars trailed at the half, 14-7. 

A Detmer to Chuck Cutler pass tied the game in the 
third quarter but Colorado took a 17-14 lead early in 
the final quarter. 

The Cougars then moved from their own four to the 
Colorado 14 and tied the game with 4:11 left on a 31- 
yarder by Chaffetz. Three plays later, Peterson's in- 
terception and a 15-yard penalty put the ball at the 
Colorado 32. With 2:33 left, Chaffetz booted a 35-yar- 
der for the victory. 

A key performer for BYU was halfback Matt Bellini, 
who carried eight times for 78 yards and caught four 
passes for 41 yards. Asad note for the Buffs came 
early in 1989 when quarterback Sal Aunese was 
hopisitalized with stomach cancer. He died during the 
1989 season, when Colorado went unbeaten during 
the regular season and went into the Orange Bowl 
ranked number-one in the country.

All American Bowl, 1987 
Virginia 22, BYU 16 
December 22 
Birmingham 
The 11th annual All American Bowl was cool 
(temperature in the high 40s after two days of rain) 
and the Cougars never got warmed up despite taking 
a 3-0 lead. 


73 


BYU's 10th consecutive bowl appearance saw 
several records set but the Cougars were edged, 22- 
16, in their initial game with Virginia. Sean Covey at- 
tempted 61 passes, David Miles had 188 yards in 
receptions and Pat Thompson only had to punt once-- 
all bowl records--but the Cougars fell just short 
several times. 

Covey, who had led the Cougars to five straight 
wins as a starter, thought he had a first down at the 
BYU 46 with four minutes remaining but the ball was 
marked just short on a fourth-down play. 

Darren Handley, BYU's leading receiver for the 
season, dropped a touchdown pass in the first quarter 
before BYU settled for a 20-yard field goal by 
Leonard Chitty. However, Chitty, who set a season 
mark for field goals (18) and consecutive PATs (29), 
missed 51 and 47-yarders in the second quarter as 
the Cavaliers took a 14-3 halftime lead. 

BYU got to the Virginia two-yard line midway 
through the third quarter only to have a fourth-down 
pass tipped at the last second. Kyle Whittingham 
scampered eight yards to cut the lead to 14-9 at the 
end of the third quarter. 

Virginia, behind quarterback Scott Secules, the 
game MVP, made a crucial 76-yard drive to go ahead, 
22-9, with 11 minutes left. BYU then went the same 
distance to score on a one-yard pass to Whittingham 
with seven minutes remaining. 

The Cougars were hurt by the absence of running 
back/returner Mike O’Brien and linebacker Thor 
Salanoa, who both suffered knee injuries in the 
Australia game. Salanoa’s loss was magnified when 
linebacker Richard Hobbs was sidelined in the first 
half of the bowl game. 

Ironically, nine years earlier on the same date (his 
wedding anniversary), Virginia Coach George Welsh 
had led his Navy team to a 23-16 win over BYU in the 
inaugural Holiday Bowl.

Freedom Bow! Ill, 1986 


UCLA 31, BYU 10 
December 30 
Anaheim 
Although BYU failed to win the WAC title, the 

Cougars kept alive an impressive streak of nine bow! 
appearances in a row by being invited to the third an- 
nual Freedom Bowl. There they met familiar foes 
UCLA and Gaston Green. 


The two teams had met in 1985 when the Bruins 
came from behind to win a closely contested struggle 
in Provo, 27-24. Green, UCLA's outstanding tailback, 
was limited to 59 yards in that game and much of 
UCLA's offense came as a result of John Lee's four 
field goals. 

The Freedom Bowl, however, proved different as 
UCLA and Green broke open a close game in the 
third quarter and went on to win 31-10. He finished 
with 266 yards on 33 carries. 

The first half was marked by defensive struggle. 
The two teams punted nine times in the first 30 
minutes with the only scores coming after a turnover 
and a trick play. 

BYU struck first. Linebacker Andy Katoa recovered 
a UCLA fumble at the Bruin 47 early in the first 
quarter, and after a Bob Jensen to Mark Bellini pass, 
Leonard Chitty put the Cougars ahead 3-0 with a 32- 
yard field goal. Soon afterward, UCLA stormed 
ahead to stay with a 71-yard touchdown drive high- 
lighted by a 49-yard run on a reverse by Bruin 
receiver Karl Dorrell. 

From there on out, it was UCLA most of the way. 
Midway into the third quarter, Bruin fullback David 
Greenwood dashed 70 yards through the BYU 
defense and set up Green's one-yard touchdown run. 
David Franey followed with a 49-yard UCLA field goal 
and later, Green bolted 79-yards with 52 seconds left 
in the period. 

Cougar quarterbacks Bob Jensen and Mike Young 
managed to pass for over 200 yards, but UCLA and 
Green did most of the damage. Costly injuries to 
BYU standouts Mark Bellini (ankle) and Andy Katoa 
(knee) also hindered the Cougar effort. On the eve- 
ning, Jensen completed 18 passes in 31 attempts for 
124 yards with three interceptions, and Young was on 
target seven times in 12 attempts for 97 yards.

BYU 3 0 0 7 10 
UCLA BYU 
Rushes- Yards 49-423 43-73 
Passing Yards 95 221 
Passes 8-21-1 25-43-3 


Florida Citrus Bowl, 1985 


Ohio State 10, BYU 7 
December 28 
Orlando 
In what promised to be an offensive showdown be- 

tween pass-happy BYU (11-2) and rough and tumble 
Ohio State (8-3), two of the nation’s top defenses 
took center stage. In fact, the game’s only touch- 
downs were produced by a noseguard on an intercep- 
tion and a reserve wide receiver on a broken play. 


The rest of the contest was marked by intense hitting, 
and as is the case with most defensive struggles, the 
team that made the fewest mistakes won the ball 
game. 

Buckeye kicker Rich Spangler tallied the first points 
of the game with a 47-yard field goal early in the 
second quarter to give Ohio State a 3-0 lead. BYU at- 
tempted to answer with a long drive of its own, only to 
come away empty after Vai Sikahema fumbled on an 
apparent touchdown plunge. 

Before the first half ended, however, the Cougars 
again mounted a long drive and finally hit paydirt 
when quarterback Robbie Bosco rolled left, whirled 
right and found wide receiver David Miles on a 38- 
yard scoring strike to give the Cougars a 7-3 halftime 
lead. 

When the teams returned to the field for the second 
half, misfortune immediately struck the Cougars. 
Deep in his own territory, Bosco attempted to throw a 
short pass underneath the dropping Buckeye 
linebackers and instead found Ohio State noseguard 
Larry Kolic who snatched the errant pass and took it 
in for an OSU touchdown and a 10-7 lead. 

On the day, Bosco was 26 for 50 and a touchdown, 
but miscues told the story. Ohio State was the 
benefactor of six Cougar turnovers, and the Big-10 
Buckeyes went home the victor.

Holiday Bowl VII, 1984 
BYU 24, Michigan 17 
December 21 
San Diego 

Holiday Bowl Vil lived up to all the glamour and ex- 
citement of the previous six bowls, plus it had a new 
dimension--national championship luster--thanks to 
BYU's undefeated, untied season and No. 1 ranking. 
The 24-17 Cougar victory marked the first time in 
years that a non-New Year's Day bowl game deter- 
mined the national championship. 

The contest was played in typical Holiday Bow! 
fashion. After a scoreless first quarter in which 
Cougar quarterback Robbie Bosco was forced to the 
sideline with an injured knee and ankle, the game 
began to develop drama. 

BYU scored first with 8:37 left in the first half on a 
five-yard run by Kelly Smith. The touchdown was set 
up when a limping Bosco returned to the lineup and 


engineered an 80-yard scoring drive. 

The Wolverines responded by putting together an 
80-yard drive of their own, culminating on Rick 
Rogers’ five-yard touchdown run up the middle. The 
PAT tied the score at 7-7. The tie was short-lived, 
however, as a 31-yard field goal by Lee Johnson with 


time running out in the first half gave BYU a 10-7 lead. 


Midway through the third period, Michigan went on 
top for the first time at 14-10 on a 10-yard scoring 
strike from Bob Zurbrugg to Bob Perryman. The Wol- 
verines added to their lead when Bob Bergeron nailed 
a 32-yard field goal to make it 17-10 with 14:14 to 
play in the game. The Cougar offense then produced 
an 80-yard drive that culminated with a seven-yard 
Bosco-to-Glen Kozlowski touchdown pass to tie the 
game at 17-17 with 10:51 to go. 

Bosco's 13-yard scoring toss to Smith with 1:23 
remaining made it 24-17 and clinched a 13-0 season 
and the Cougars’ first-ever national championship.

Holiday Bowl VI, 1983 
BYU 21, Missouri 17 
December 23 
San Diego 

Yet another chapter was added to the saga of im- 
possible, thrilling Holiday Bowl finishes when BYU 
defeated Missouri, 21-17. The game was billed as a 
classic matchup between Missouri's stubborn 
defense and BYU's explosive offense, led by quarter- 
back Steve Young. 

For most of the game the defenses were in control. 
Missouri held BYU to just 167 yards of total offense in 
the first half and gave up only one touchdown to the 
Cougars. BYU's defense, however, allowed the 
Tigers only 157 yards of offense and 10 points in the 
first half. Missouri got its first-half points on a two- 
yard run by Eric Drain and a 37-yard field goal by 
Brad Burditt. 

BYU’s touchdown came on a 10-yard quarterback 
draw by Young. In the second half, Young hit Eddie 
Stinnett with a 33-yard pass. But Missouri came right 
back with another two-yard plunge by Drain. A valiant 
goal-line stand by BYU's defense gave the offense 
the ball on the six yard line with 3:57 left in the game. 

The Cougars marched down field until, with a first- 
and-10 situation on the Missouri 15 yard line, Young 


handed the ball to Stinnett, who faked a sweep and 
lofted the ball back to Young. The ball barely cleared 
the outstretched hands of Missouri's Bobby Bell, but 
Young hauled in the throw and rambled into the end 
zone. The extra point gave BYU the lead 21-17 with 
only 23 seconds left. 

Kyle Morrell then intercepted a Missouri despera- 
tion pass. Casey Tiumalu picked up 57 yards on 27 
carries.

Holiday Bowl V, 1982 
Ohio State 47, BYU 17 
December 17 
San Diego 

The Cougars had their two-game Holiday Bowl win 
streak snapped by Ohio State, 47-17. 

The BYU bubble burst at the outset of the third 
quarter, when the Buckeyes reeled off 17 un- 
answered points, while BYU only got one snap--an 
interception at that. The OSU damage came mainly 
on the ground, as the Buckeyes won their seventh 
consecutive game. 

Tim Spencer was the main gun for the Bucks, as 
he blew through the Cougar defense for 167 yards, in- 
cluding a record-setting 61-yard touchdown run in the 
second quarter that put OSU up 10-7. 

As usual, the BYU quarterback rolled up impressive 
marks, but the Cougars had trouble making points 
out of Steve Young’s 341 yards passing on 27 of 45 
accuracy. The Cougars turned the ball over to OSU 
three times inside the 30 and saw the Bucks convert 
each turnover into a score. 

The Cougars’ two touchdowns were at each end of 
the game, with the first, a seven-yard strike to Neil 
Balholm, coming in the opening quarter and the final 
tally, a 16-yard Young-to-Hudson connection.

Holiday Bowl IV, 1981 
BYU 38, Washington State 36 
December 18 
San Diego 

The 1981 edition of the Holiday Bowl fit the mold of 
its predecessors. This time it was the Cougars of 
BYU versus the Cougars of Washington State playing 
before a crowd of 52,419. Again it was like two dif- 
ferent games. BYU held a 24-7 lead at halftime, then 
pushed the score to 31-7, as Tom Holmoe intercepted 
a pass and returned it 35 yards to give the WAC team 
a comfortable 31-7 edge. 

But as the second half progressed, it appeared 
BYU would be the come-from-behind victim in this 
one. With only 59 seconds left in the third period, 
WSU brought the score to 31-28, and from there the 
team from Provo had to hold on for its life. 

In a crucial third-and-12 situation on BYU’s 18 yard 
line with 30 seconds remaining in the third period, Mc- 
Mahon began completing passes that covered 82 
yards and put BYU in the end zone again to stretch 
the lead to 38-28. 

Statistically, it was a typical McMahon game: 27 of 
43 with no interceptions, 342 yards and three touch- 
downs. But he will be remembered most, perhaps, 
for turning a fumbled snap into a carry for a first 
down. 

Tight end Gordon Hudson had seven receptions for 


126 yards anda TD.

Holiday Bowl Ill, 1980 
BYU 46, Southern Methodist 45 
December 19 
San Diego 

One observer said the 1980 Holiday Bowl was ac- 
tually two ball games--the first a contest that lasted 
most of four quarters, the other that was of four 
minutes duration. SMU seemed in control the first 
game, while the Cougars, behind 20 points with only 
minutes remaining, surged to a stunning 46-45 win. 

Unfortunately, many fans either turned off their TV 
sets or left the stadium in San Diego before the 
"second game" began. For 56 minutes the Mustangs 
taught the Cougars a lesson in running the football. 
The Mustang's two great runners, Craig James and 
Eric Dickerson, rushed for a combined total of 335 


yards. 

With 4:07 remaining in the game, SMU scored to 
go ahead, 45-25. From there, the Cougars con- 
nected on a TD pass, recovered an on-side kick, 
scored on a one-yard scamper by Scoit Phillips, 
blocked a punt and, finally (with three seconds left on 
the clock), scored on a “Hail Mary" pass of 46 yards 
from Jim McMahon to Clay Brown. With the score 
tied, Kurt Gunther kicked the extra point to send BYU 
fans streaming onto the field. 

Statistically, McMahon completed 32 of 49 passes 
for 446 yards. It may have been the most exciting 
bowl game ever with the Cougars getting 21 points in 
the final two minutes and 33 seconds.

Holiday Bowl Il, 1979 
Indiana 38, BYU 37 
December 21 
San Diego 

The 1979 season ended with BYU leading the na- 
tion in three major categories: scoring, passing and 
total offense. Moreover, the Cougars were one of the 
few undefeated, untied teams in the nation with an 11- 
0 mark. 

Those kinds of credentials, plus their national rank- 
ings in the polls (9th UPI and AP), made the Mountain 
Cats favorites over the at-large entry, Indiana. It was 
BYU's first-ever meeting with a Big Ten school, and 
the net result was one of the most exciting bowl 
games of the season. 

The opportunistic Hoosiers played BYU even in the 
first quarter, then carried a 21-17 lead at the half. 
BYU spurted ahead in the third period, 34-31, on a 15- 
yard pass from All-American Marc Wilson to fullback 
Eric Lane. 

But midway through the fourth quarter, Indiana's 
Tim Wilber returned a punt 62 yards to push the 
Hoosiers ahead 38-37. With 2:06 remaining, the 
Cougars moved the ball 69 yards to the Indiana 10 
yard line. 

Then, with 11 seconds left, Brent Johnson missed a 
27-yard field goal that would have made the score 40- 
38. Marc Wilson was voted the MVP on offense (28 
of 43 for 380 yards) and Indiana's cornerback, Tim 
Wilber, won the honor on defense. Attendance: 
52,200.

Holiday Bowl, 1978 


Navy 21, BYU 16 
December 22 
San Diego 

The inaugural game of the Holiday Bowl pitted two 
teams with local drawing power, the Naval Academy 
and Brigham Young. Despite chilly temperatures, (for 
San Diego that is), the two teams engaged in a hard- 
hitting battle. 

The Cougars had a 16-3 lead at one point, even 
though the score was 9-3 at the end of the first half. 
Defense dominated the first two quarters. Brent 
Johnson put the Cougars on the scoreboard first with 
a 33-yard field goal with 8:26 left in the first quarter. 
But Navy's Bob Tata boomed through a 40-yard field 
goal to tie the score at 3-3. 

Jim McMahon sent the Cougars ahead with a 10- 
yard pass to Mike Chronister, but Johnson missed the 
extra point. McMahon ran another in from two-yards 
out in the third period before the Middies took 
charge. Navy scored two field goals in the fourth 
quarter, but it was a 65-yard pass to Phil McConkey 
that did the Cougars in. 

Some 48,003 witnessed the game in person. Mc- 
Conkey was named Best Offensive Player, BYU's 
Tom Enlow won Best Defensive Player honors for the 
game. Navy finished the year with a 9-3 record, BYU 
with a 9-4 mark.

Tangerine Bowl, 1976 
Oklahoma State 49, BYU 21 
December 18 
Orlando 

Powerful Oklahoma State, sparked by All-American 
Terry Miller (173 yards rushing on 23 carries), used 
four pass interceptions and a fumble recovery to cap- 
ture the Tangerine Bowl contest. Defensively, the 
Cowboys yielded only 46 yards rushing. 

The Cougars, however, stayed close in the first 
half, which ended 28-14. But Dave Lowry returned 


77 


the second-half kickoff 102 yards (a Tangerine Bowl 
record) to bring the score to 21-28. The crowd of 
37,812 fans saw the two teams roll up 70 points be- 
tween them. 

Quarterback Gifford Nielsen completed 23 of 34 
passes for 209 yards and one touchdown, but the 
Cougars could only roll up 255 total yards to 402 for 
OSU. BYU's record was 9-2 prior to the Tangerine 
Bowl game. 

Oklahoma State had tied for the Big Eight title but 
missed out on the Orange Bowl due to the league’s 
tie-breaking formula.

Fiesta Bowl, 1974 
Oklahoma State 16, BYU 6 
December 28 
Tempe 

Competing in its first post-season bowl game ever, 
BYU played Western Athletic Conference host to the 
Cowboys of Oklahoma State before a crowd of 
50,878. 

The Cougars got off to a quick start with two field 
goals during the first quarter before All-WAC quarter- 
back Gary Sheide was injured in the last moments of 
the first quarter. He was replaced by junior reserve 
Mark Giles. 

The Cowboys took the scoring lead during the 
second quarter on a TD run around left end, so the 
halftime score was OSU 7, BYU 6. OSU regained 
possession of the ball on the Cougars’ 17 yard line 
after intercepting a Giles-to-Tom Toolson pass, but 
was unable to convert on the turnover. 

A field goal from 32 yards out put the Cowboys in 
front 10-7 at the end of the third quarter. With 1:14 
remaining in the game, OSU scored another TD ona 
40-yard halfback pass to complete the scoring. 

The Cougars outgained the Cowboys in total yard- 
age 301 to 214. Giles was 11 out of 26 for 138 
yards. BYU's record was 7-3-1 prior to the Fiesta 
Bowl.

Historical View 


Being recognized 
as one of the BYU and the Heisman Trophy 
country’s top Year Player Finish 
ee one 1D 1962 Eldon Fortie 10th 
mention winning. | 965 vig carer 1h 
ship--seemed out of 1974 Gary Sheide 8th 
the realm of pos- 1976 Gifford Nielsen 6th 
sibility for BYU. 1979 Mare Wilson 3rd 

After all, it was 26 | 1980 Jim McMahon 5th 
years ago that the 1981 Jim McMahon 3rd 
Cougars won their 1983 Steve Young 2nd 
first Western Ath- 1984  RobbieBosco 3rd 
letic Conference 1985 Robbie Bosco 3rd 
football title. And 4989 Ty Detmer oth 
the team had 
managed only 171 1990 Ty Detmer 1st 
victories and 16 win- 


ning seasons 
before the 1972 season. 

It was in 1972, however, that BYU's football pro- 
gram shifted gears. LaVell Edwards was named head 
coach in that year, and things have not been the 
same since. The Cougars have won 175 games and 
been to 15 bowl games. Thirteen times BYU has won 
the WAC title under Edwards’ direction, including 10 
in a row from 1976-85. And the Cougars won the na- 
tional championship in 1984. 

Things weren't always so rosy in BYU's program, 
however. Football got its beginning at BYU in 1922. 
That first season, BYU won only one game and 
scored a total of 10 points during the season. Now, 
BYU is traditionally one of the nation’s leaders in scor- 
ing and offense. 

Football was introduced at BYU near the beginning 
of the century, but a fatality prompted its discon- 
tinuance. The game returned in 1922 and, minus 
three years during World War II, the Cougars have 
played a total of 67 seasons. BYU initially joined 
ranks with the old Rocky Mountain Conference and 
later became part of the Mountain States, Skyline and 
Western Athletic Conferences. 

Some of the more noted athletes who have helped 
propel BYU into the national limelight have been 
quarterbacks. Virgil Carter (1966), Gary Sheide 
(1974), Gifford Nielsen (1976), Marc Wilson (1979), 
Jim McMahon (1981), Steve Young (1983) and Rob- 
bie Bosco (1984-85) have all left their mark on 
BYU's record books. And Ty Detmer (1988- ) is still 
leaving his. 

Nielsen, Wilson, McMahon, Young and Bosco were 


All-American quarterbacks, and each erased marks 
set by his predecessor. McMahon, Wilson and Bosco 
were third-place finishers in the Heisman Trophy bal- 
loting, while Steve Young finished second. Both Mc- 
Mahon and Young were recipients of the Davey 
O'Brien Award, given to the outstanding collegiate 
quarterback. 

While passing has been the name of the game at 
BYU since Edwards took over, there also have been 
some impressive running backs. Eldon "The Phan- 
tom" Fortie (1961-62), a single-wing back, won All- 
America honors in 1962. "Fleet Pete" 
VanValkenburg (1970-71), led the nation rushing his 
senior year and was second-team All-America. 

Fullback John Ogden (1964-66) was the leading 
rusher in the WAC for three consecutive years. And 
fullback Todd Christensen (1975-77) did such a 
good job running with and catching the football he 
later became an all-pro tight end. He was the leading 
receiver on BYU's team for three years. Lakei 
Heimuli (1983-86) is the school’s rushing leader. 

A good passing team must have good personnel on 
the offensive line, and BYU has had its share of 
honors recipients in that area. 

Mohammed Elewonibi (1988-89) won the Outland 
Trophy in 1989, BYU's second such honor in four 
years. Other line standouts include Gordon Gravelle 
(1970), Paul Howard (1972), Brad Oates (1973-75), 
Orrin Olsen (1972-75), Tom Bell (1974-79), Nick 
Eyre (1977-80), Bart Oates (1977-82) and Trevor 
Matich (1979-84). 

There also have been several outstanding tight 
ends at BYU, most recently Clay Brown (1978-80), 
Gordon Hudson (1979-83) David Mills (1980-84), 
Trevor Molini (1985-86) and Chris Smith (1987-90). 

Top defensive players include Wayne Baker (1972- 
74), Paul Linford (1972-74), Mekeli leremia (1975- 
77), Glen Titensor (1978-80), Glen Redd (1976-80), 
Kyle Whittingham (1979-81), Todd Shell (1980-83), 
Kyle Morrell (1980-84) and Jason Buck (1985-86). 
Buck, the 1986 recipient of the Outland Trophy, was 
only the third player in the western United States to 
win the award. 

At various times in the last 20 years, the Cougars 
have had some great return specialists. Chris 
Farasopoulos (1968-70), the Galloping Greek, led 
the nation in punt returns and set two NCAA records. 
Golden Richards (1970-71) is still listed as a holder 
of several NCAA records. Vai Sikahema (1980-85) 
set an NCAA record for most punt returns and later 
earned All-Pro status with the Cardinals.

‘Bie 


All-Sports Winner 


For the 25th time in the 29-year history of the Western 
Athletic Conference, BYU has won the annual men’s 
all-sports trophy. 

The Cougars, who have won the honor 14 consecu- 
tive years, earned league crowns in cross country, foot- 
ball, indoor and outdoor track and golf. BYU scored 67 
points to win the mythical trophy, followed by New Mex- 
ico with 48, Utah with 42, Air Force 39, San Diego State 
37.5, Wyoming 36, UTEP 34.5, Hawaii 28.5 and Colo- 
rado State 21.5. 

New Mexico claimed a pair of first place finishes in 
gymnastics and tennis. Utah finished first in basketball 
and swimming. Air Force finished first in wrestling and 
Hawaii was first in baseball. 

BYU finished second in basketball and gymnastics, 
third in baseball, fourth in wrestling and fifth in tennis. 

National finishes by the football, golf and indoor and 
outdoor track were indicators of BYU's overall athletic 
strength. 

In football, the Cougars finished 17th in the final 
United Press International poll. BYU, who was ranked 
as high as fourth in both wire service polls after a 4-0 
start, finished with a 10-3 record and defended the WAC 
title. It was the 14th WAC football title for BYU and its 
13th in the 19-year rein of Coach LaVell Edwards. 

Golf took the first round lead in the NCAA Champi- 
onships at Pebble Beach, Calif., with an all-junior team. 
The Cougars, who had missed qualifying the last three 
seasons for the NCAA, placed fifth in that tourney after 
winning their 18th WAC title in the 30-year rein of Coach 
Karl Tucker. 

Sprinter Frank Fredericks, a 13-time All-American, 
was largely responsible for BYU's eighth place finish in 


indoor track at Indianapolis, Ind., and a fourth place 
finish in outdoor track at Eugene, Ore. Indoors, Fred- 
ericks won the 200 meters and was third in the 55 
meters. 

Outdoors, the Cougar senior became the first sprinter 
since 1978 to win both the 100 and 200 meters. Indoors 
BYU defended its title and won the WAC crown for the 
eighth time. Outdoors it was the 11th title for the Cou- 
gars, the seventh in a row. 

Other track All-Americans in addition to Fredericks 
were: Brent Patera (fourth in discus), Per Karlsson (third 
inthe hammer), and the 4 X 100 relay team of Jim Waite, 
Erik Hughes and Oluyemi Kayode which Fredericks 
anchored to a fifth place finish. 

Heisman Trophy winner Ty Detmer was a consensus 
All-America, being named to six first-teams and tight 
end Chris Smith was a unanimous All-American. 

Junior Jason Brown placed fifth in the floor exercise 
to earn All-America honors in gymnastics. Center 
Shawn Bradley, 7-6, was an honorable mention All- 
America by Associated Press and WAC Freshman of 
the Year. Middle blocker Ethan Watts was named to the 
all-freshman team by Volleyball Monthly. And Cougar 
quarterback-pitcher Ryan Hancock was named WAC 
Freshman of the Year in baseball. 

The Cougars’ Academic All-Americans from the 
men’s teams were Andy Boyce (football) Carl Hanson 
(cross country) and Doug Hobbs (cross country). 

Although the Cougar basketball, 21-13, team finished 
second in the WAC regular season, they did win the 
league post-season tournament and secured the 
school’s first automatic berth in the NCAA post-season 
tournament.

Game-By-Game Summary 


BYU 30, UTEP 10 


Sept. 1 1990--night game (89 degrees, partly 
cloudy, 40% humidity and 15 mph wind) at the Sun 
Bowl in El Paso, Texas (29,033) 


The Cougars got off to a good start offensively in 
the first quarter after the BYU defense forced the 
Miners to punt on the first possession of the game. Ty 
Detmer led the Cougars from their own 35 to the 
Miner two-yard line before capping the drive with a 
dive. The Miners came right back when Miner tailback 
Larry Sims carried from 12 yards out for the touch- 
down making the score 7-7. UTEP scored on a 44- 
yard field goal to start the second quarter for the only 
UTEP lead of the night, 10-7. Touchdown runs of 
three yards by Detmer and one yard by Peter 
Tuipulotu gave the Cougars a 21-10 halftime lead. An 
Earl Kauffman 24-yard field goal was all the scoring in 
the third quarter. Detmer hit Nati Valdez from 28 
yards and a touchdown late in the fourth quarter. 
Kauffman’s kick was blocked leaving the final score 
30-10. Cougar Coach LaVell Edwards said "I think 
our defense is headed in the right direction." Edwards 
also added "It was a typical opening night with lots of 


mistakes but I’m satisfied with the victory." BYU is 1-0.

BYU 28, Miami 21 
September 8, 1990--night game (93 degrees, fair 
and hot with a slight wind) at Cougar Stadium, Provo 
(66,235-stadium record) 


Ty Detmer was showcased to the nation by an 
ESPN national telecast and the defense rose to the 
occasion against the defending national champion 
and top-ranked team. Miami started the scoring mid- 
way through the first quarter on a Stephen McGuire 
seven-yard run. Detmer then led the Cougars on a 60- 
yard, seven-play drive. Matt Bellini caught a 14-yard 
touchdown pass and Earl Kauffman’s PAT tied the 
score at seven. Kauffman's 32-yard field goal with 
nine minutes left in the second quarter made it, 10-7, 
BYU. After a BYU turnover, Miami took the ball from 
the Cougar 43-yard line and scored on another Mc- 
Guire run, of two yards, to lead, 14-10, with 1:42 in 
the half. Detmer then completed seven straight pas- 
ses and marched the Cougars to the Miami two-yard 
line. He hit Andy Boyce for the touchdown and with 
the PAT, the Cougars went into halftime with a 17-14 
lead, Miami quarterback Craig Erickson came out hot 
and led his team on an 80-yard drive that was capped 
by a Leonard Conley seven-yard run to open the 
second half. It gave the Hurricanes a 21-17 lead but 
that was all the points they would get. BYU narrowed 
the gap to 21-20 on a 29-yard Kauffman field goal 
with 9:26 remaining in the third quarter. With eight 
minutes left in the third, Miami elected to go for it ona 
fourth and one. McGuire was stopped for no gain by 
Rich Kaufusi and the ball went over to BYU. From 
there, Detmer directed a nine play, 43-yard drive that 
ended with a seven-yard touchdown pass to Mike 
Salido. BYU converted the two point conversion on a 
pass from Detmer to Boyce. Twice, late in the fourth 
quarter, Miami threatened but was held by the BYU 
defense. Ervin Lee intercepted an Erickson pass in 
the end zone to halt one drive and then after the 
Cougars stuffed Miami on a third and two, Lee broke 
up a fourth down pass and sealed the victory. BYU is 
now 2-0.

BYU 50 , Washington State 36 


September 15, 1990--day game (88 degrees, partly 
cloudy) at Cougar Stadium, Provo (65,848) 


Washington State was coming off a big loss the pre- 
vious week at Wyoming and came out hungry. A 49- 
yard pass play, a 42-yard run and a pair of Jason 
Hanson field goals gave WSU a 20-7 lead after one 
quarter. BYU's only score came on a four-yard touch- 
down pass from Ty Detmer to Peter Tuipulotu. Han- 
son converted on another kick in the second quarter 
and Alvin Dunn picked off a Detmer pass and 
returned it 25 yards for the score. WSU's two point try 
failed, but it still took a 29-7 lead into halftime. The 
Cougars took the second half kickoff and marched 73 
yards for a touchdown to open the third quarter, the 
score coming on a Detmer to Nati Valdez two-yard 
pass. That would be all the scoring in the third quarter 
as WSU had a 29-14 lead going into the final period. 
Detmer and the Cougar offense got rolling and the 
defense kept getting the ball back for them. Detmer 
threw two touchdown passes, 16 yards to Brent 
Nyberg and 32 yards to Andy Boyce. BYU tied the 
score at 29-29 when Chris Smith dove into the end 
zone with the two-point conversion after the Boyce 
touchdown. On the next series, WSU was forced to 
punt. The Cougar drive started on the 16 and 13 


96 


plays later Detmer found Stacey Corley who 
scampered in from the nine-yard line. It was Detmer’s 
fifth touchdown pass of the game and made the score 
36-29. WSU came right back with a five play, 80-yard 
drive and scored on a seven-yard touchdown pass 
that tied the score at 36. BYU put the game away with 
touchdown runs of 23 yards by Tuipulotu and five 
yards by Corley in the final minutes that made the 
final score 50-36. BYU is now 3-0.

BYU 62, San Diego State 34 
September 22, 1990--day game (78 degrees, few 
scattered clouds and a slight breeze) at Cougar 
Stadium, Provo (66,044) 


In a game telecast nationally by CBS, Ty Detmer 
threw for 514 yards in leading the Cougars to their 
fourth straight win. Early on it was evident that it 
would be a big offensive day for both squads. BYU 
took a 21-0 lead early in the second quarter, but 
SDSU came roaring back on the arm of quarterback 
Dan McGwire to score 24 second-quarter points. 

BYU got another touchdown to give the Cougars a 28- 
21 halftime lead. Touchdown runs by Cougar running 


backs in the second half would be the difference in 
this game. BYU rushed 24 times and scored on six of 
those carries. One of the scores was set up when an 
Aztec punt was blocked by defensive back Derwin 
Gray, giving BYU possession on the Aztec one-yard 
line. Detmer had three touchdown passes on the day, 
all of them going to wide receiver Andy Boyce. SDSU 
scored on a second half field goal and a one-yard TD 
pass which made the final score 62-34 for BYU. Two 
stadium records were set by the Aztecs; most passes 
attempted by an individual (59 by McGwire) and most 
passes attempted by a team (62 by SDSU). Matt Bel- 
lini moved into second place on the Cougar all-time 
reception list with his nine catches, leaving him four 
shy of the record held by Phil Odle. BYU is now 2-0 in 
the WAC and 4-0 overall.

Oregon 32, BYU 16 


September 29, 1990--day game (70 degrees, 
sunny with a 10-15 mph wind) at Autzen Stadium, 
Eugene (45,022) 


Oregon jumped out to a first quarter lead of 12-0. 


97 


The Ducks' Bill Musgrave connected with Sean Bur- 
well for a 31-yard touchdown pass, Ty Detmer was 
sacked in his own end zone for a safety and Oregon 
kicker Gregg McCallum split the uprights from 26 
yards to give the Ducks the early advantage. The 
Cougars got on the board midway through the second 
quarter when Detmer, who hadn't practiced all week 
due to a ligament sprain in his throwing hand, hit 
reserve running back Scott Charlton on a-two yard 
scoring pass. Earl Kauffman’s 40-yard field goal at 
the start of the third quarter cut the lead to 12-10, but 
that is as close as it would get. Oregon scored on 
touchdown passes of 12 and 30 yards in the third 
quarter then got one more touchdown in the final 
period when Musgrave scored on a three-yard 
plunge. One bright spot for the Cougars came in the 
fourth quarter when Detmer and Micah Matsuzaki 
hooked up on a 69-yard touchdown pass. It was the 
longest Cougar pass play so far this year. Matt Bellini 
had five catches and moved past Phil Odle as the all- 
time Cougar leader in pass receptions. Odle’s pre- 
vious mark was 183, Bellini now has 185. The game 
was telecast by ABC Sports with Brent Musburger 
and Dick Vermeil calling the action. BYU is now 4-1 
overall and 2-0 in the WAC but drops from fourth to 
ninth and 10th in UPI and AP.

BYU 52, Colorado State 9 


October 13, 1990--day game (71 degrees, sunny 
and no wind) at Cougar Stadium, Provo (65,922) 


After a bye week, the Cougars used a good 
balance of passing and rushing to run away with this 
one from the start. BYU took a commanding 31-7 
lead into halftime, mostly on account of the running 
backs and a stingy defense. Peter Tuipulotu, Mike 
Salido and Scott Charlton all caught touchdown pas- 
ses from Ty Detmer in the first half and Cougar 
rushers amassed 117 yards by the break. They 
finished the game with 197 yards on the ground. Det- 
mer broke the NCAA record for most consecutive 
games with 300 yards total offense. His 319 yards 
gave him 13 straight which surpassed the record of 
12 set by Jim McMahon in 1980-81. Brian Mitchell led 
the defense with two interceptions, one of which he 
returned 49 yards for a touchdown. Scott Giles who 
gave up his redshirt plans to replace the injured Jared 
Leavitt, had six tackles on the day. Detmer, despite 
his lowest passing yardage (316) as a starter, ex- 
tended his NCAA record of 300-yard games passing 
to 18 to tie Steve Young's NCAA record set from 1981- 
83. BYU is now 5-1 overall and 3-0 in the WAC.

BYU 55, New Mexico 31 


Oct. 27, 1990--day game (66 degrees, clear with a 
slight wind) at Cougar Stadium, Provo (66,086) 


Homecoming 1990 will be a day long remembered 
by Cougar standouts Ty Detmer, Andy Boyce and Der- 
win Gray. Detmer broke Steve Young’s NCAA record 
for most games with over 300 yards total offense. 
This was Detmer’s 19th outing in which he has 
amassed over 300 yards. Andy Boyce had a career 
day receiving with nine catches for 235 yards and two 
touchdowns. Derwin Gray picked off three Lobo pas- 
ses and returned one of them 17 yards for a touch- 
down. His three interceptions ties a school record 
held by five others. New Mexico kept the game close 
until mid-way through the second quarter thanks to 
three touchdown passes by Jeremy Leach. With the 
scored tied at 21, the Cougars scored 17 unanswered 
points in the last five minutes of the half. Micah Mat- 
suzaki scored his second touchdown of the day ona 
four-yard pass from Detmer. Three minutes later, 
Gray grabbed a deflected Leach pass and took it in 
for the score. Kicker Keith Lever, subbing for the in- 
jured Earl Kauffman, was good on a 44-yard field goal 
to end the half. In the second half, Matsuzaki and 
Boyce were on the receiving end of Detmer TD pas- 
ses. Kauffman, who had started the game but injured 
his elbow on a roughing-the-punter play, returned in 
the third quarter to kick a 38-yard field goal. Repre- 
sentatives from the Sea World Holiday, Florida Citrus, 
Anaheim Freedom and Japan Bowls were on hand to 
scout BYU. BYU is now 6-1 overall and 4-0 in the 


WAC.

BYU 54, Air Force 7 


November 3, 1990--day game (27 degrees, blow- 
ing snow with 25 mph winds) at Falcon Stadium, 
Colorado Springs, Colorado (38,705) 


Ty Detmer and the BYU offense heated up a chilly 
Falcon field by scoring the game's first 37 points. 
After taking a 17-0 lead into halftime the Cougars put 
the game away with a 30 point third quarter. For the 
second straight week Detmer and Boyce proved to be 
a deadly combination as Boyce caught 10 passes for 
127-yards and 3 TD’s, giving him 10 for the season. 
Despite the frozen conditions Detmer threw for 397 
yards in three quarters, tying him with Steve Young 
for a NCAA record of most consecutive games with 
200 or more yards passing. It was Detmer’s 22nd 
NCAA record either held or shared. The defense was 
equally impressive grounding the Falcon running at- 
tack all afternoon. Tony Crutchfield led the defense 
with a forced fumble and a 37-yard interception return 
for a touchdown. BYU is now 7-1 overall and 5-0 in 


the WAC.

BYU 45, Wyoming 14 
November 10, 1990--day game (50 degrees, most- 
ly sunny, 15-25 mph winds) at War Memorial 
Stadium, Laramie, Wyoming (34,231) 


In a game played before a national television 
audience and the first sellout in Wyoming history, the 
Cougars all but wrapped up another WAC champion- 
ship with a 45-14 victory. Ty Detmer returned to the 
sight of his worst college performance and passed for 
484-yards and two TD’s. As a freshman in 1988, Det- 
mer left the field after throwing four interceptions 
against the Cowboys; this time back Detmer walked 
off with four more NCAA records. The junior also be- 
came the all-time leading passer in BYU history top- 
ping the record held by Jim McMahon. The Cougars 
jumped out to a 24-0 lead in the second before Tom 
Corontoz brought Wyoming back with two straight 
scoring drives. However, the Cougars would send 
the sellout crowd home disappointed as Peter 
Tuipulotu scored three times and Stacey Corley 
added a fourth. The Cougars rolled up 679-yards of 
total offense.

BYU 45, Utah 22 
November 17, 1990--day game (43 degrees, over- 
cast with slight breeze) at Rice Stadium, Salt Lake 
City (33,515) 


BYU rallied from a 10-7 deficit in the first quarter to 
take a 35-10 halftime lead in winning its second 
straight Western Athletic Conference title and the 
13th in 19 years under LaVell Edwards. The win also 
clinched the 13th straight bowl appearance (Sea 
World Holiday) for the Cougars. Stacey Corley had a 
five-yard touchdown run in the first quarter and Ty 


Detmer tossed four touchdown passes in the first half. 


Detmer set two more NCAA milestones, becoming 
the first junior to pass for more than 10,000 yards and 
the first quarterback ever to have back to back 4,000- 
yard seasons. He finished the afternoon with 451 
yards and added his fifth scoring strike. Three 
Cougars--Josh Arnold, Tony Crutchfield and Brian 
Mitchell--had interceptons while Arnold and Rocky 
Biegel recovered fumbles. Utah took an early lead on 
a 56-yard bomb from Mike Richmond to Bryan 
Rowley but the Utes did get another touchdown until 
three minutes remained in the game. Wayne 
Lammle’s three field goals accounted for the rest of 


the scoring.

BYU 45, Utah State 10 


November 24, 1990--day game (50 degrees, fair 
with no wind) at Cougar Stadium, Provo (65,876) 


It was another record setting day for Cougar 
quarterback Ty Detmer. The junior broke the NCAA 
record for most passing yards in a season (4,869) set 
in 1989 by Houston’s Andre Ware (4,669). The his- 
toric pass came with just under nine minutes to play 
in the third quarter as Detmer hit Andy Boyce for 16 
yards. Detmer finished the game with 560 yards 
which moved him into second place in career passing 
yards (10,681) behind Todd Santos (11,425) of San 
Diego State. The Cougars again used a big second 
and third quarter to put away their opponent, scoring 
38 points in those quarters. Detmer threw for five 
TD's and Josh Arnold returned an interception 31 
yards for another score. The six home games broke 
stadium records for total attendance and average. 
The Cougars drew 396,011 for an average of 66,002. 
The previous high of 65,408 was set in 1985.

Hawaii 59, BYU 28 


December 1, 1990--night game (78 degrees, fair, 
65 percent humidity and six mph wind) at Aloha 
Stadium in Honolulu, Hawaii (45,729) 


An emotional day in which Ty Detmer became the 
first Heisman Trophy winner ever from BYU and the 
Western Athletic Conference seemed to take its toll 
as, for the second straight year, Hawaii jumped to a 
big early lead and dominated play. The Rainbows, be- 
hind the passing of Garrett Gabriel and the running of 
Dane McArthur, broke a 7-7 tie to lead, 28-7, after the 
first quarter and 35-14 at halftime. The Cougars 
closed to 41-28 at the end of the third quarter but the 
Rainbows reeled off 18 more points in the final 
period. Hawaii intercepted four passes, three of them 
by Kenny Harper. The Rainbows churned out 667 
yards in total offense. Detmer passed for 319 yards to 
finish with 5,188 for the season--the most ever in col- 
lege history. In the process he wound up the season 
with 42 NCAA records and a tie for five others. Andy 
Boyce caught seven passes for 106 yards and a 
school single season reception record with 1,241 
yards. Chris Smith finished the season with 68 
catches for 1,156 yards--an NCAA record for most 
yardage for a tight end. BYU finishes the regular 
season 10-2 and 7-1 in the WAC.

Now You Know 

Goal Posts Narrowed: A rule change for this 
season is narrowing the goal posts from 23 feet, 
four inches to 18 feet, six inches. The increasing ac- 
curacy and proficiency of kickers on field goals and 
extra-point attempts prompted the change. Another 
change is letting the receiving team have the ball 30 
yards downfield from the spot of a kick when a kick- 
off goes out of bounds (normally that would be the 
receiving team’s 35-yard line). However, the receiv- 
ing team can require the other team to kick over. 
Also, players are prohibited from writing words or 
numbers on their body, tape, wrist bands, etc. 

Passing Efficiency: To determine passing ef- 
ficiency rating points, multiply a player’s yards per 
attempt by 8.4, add his completion percentage, add 
his touchdown percentage multiplied by 3.3, than 
subtract his interception percentage multiplied by 2. 
Maintain the decimal point selector in the floating 
position and carry out all final perentages to two 
decimal places. Good luck! 

Talented Team: As in year’s past, the 1991 BYU 
football team consists of players who have lived in 
all parts of the world. The number of returned mis- 
sionaries in the program is 40 with another 19 cur- 
rently on missions. Of the 40, 26 went outside the 
United States. Languages spoken on the team in- 
clude Spanish, Africanz, Tahitian, Portuguese, 
Japanese, German, Samoan, Cantonese, Thai, Ton- 
gan, Italian and French. This year’s team also in- 
cludes 16 players who are married (there were five 
children at press time).
